That also covers a checkpoint deleted and registered again while a registration was under way. A storage with nothing to order positions by, such as Redis or the in-memory storage, keeps the 0.33.0 rule. See [ADR 130](doc/architecture/decisions/0130-a-subscriptions-first-position-race-resolves-by-order-not-by-write-order.md) and [#771](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/771).
* **After `DurableSubscriptionModel.subscribe(..)` throws while storing a checkpoint, a cancel or a new `subscribe(..)` of the id stops the leftover subscription's checkpoint writes.** The subscription model it wraps can throw from `subscribe(..)` and still deliver events. That leftover subscription could write a checkpoint past events the next `subscribe(..)` of the id hadn't delivered, and a restart then skipped them. Until a cancel or a later `subscribe(..)` of the id, it writes its checkpoints as before. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. See [section 7 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#7-durablesubscriptionmodel-refuses-a-first-subscription-when-no-start-position-can-be-recorded).
* **MongoDB subscriptions save their position even when no events match.** Before, the position moved only when an event matched, so after a long quiet spell a pause, a restart or a lease moving to another node resumed from a position the oplog had dropped, and got the lost-history handling although nothing was missed. `DurableSubscriptionModel` and `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` now save a quiet subscription's position once a minute, following your persist predicate. `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel` now reads the next batch only after your action has finished with the one before. See [section 19 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#a-quiet-subscriptions-checkpoint-is-written-once-a-minute), [ADR 142](doc/architecture/decisions/0142-a-quiet-mongodb-subscription-moves-its-position-from-the-empty-batch.md), [#1168](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1168) and [#1169](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1169).
* **A MongoDB subscription starting at the present no longer skips events when paused or restarted before its first event.** Before, the model only held `StartAt.now()`, so a resume started at its own time and lost the event being handled and everything written during the pause. Now `NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` and `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` record MongoDB's operation time from a `ping`, and `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel` the time of the `subscribe(..)` call, without `subscribe(..)` or `resumeSubscription(..)` waiting for it. The event being handled at the pause may arrive twice. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.7.0, and in 0.30.0 for `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel`. Resolves [#1161](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1161).
  * A question to MongoDB that fails is retried like a change stream that fails to open. When the `ping` reply has no operation time, the native and Spring models log a `WARN`, open at the present and record nothing.
* **`ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel` subscriptions that start at the present now start from the `subscribe(..)` call, so an event written just after it is no longer skipped.** A new subscription can now also receive events written up to 16 seconds before the call, and one whose change stream first opens after the oplog window gets the lost-history handling. A reactor `CheckpointAwareSubscriptionModel` of your own must implement the new `globalCheckpointAsOfNow()`. See [section 21 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call) and the `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel` javadoc for the exact limits. Fixes [#1189](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1189) and [#1192](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1192).
* **The subscription TCK's `SubscriptionModelFixture` gains `howFarBackANewSubscriptionMayStart()`, for a model that can deliver events written a little before `subscribe(..)`.** It is zero by default. `SubscriptionModelConformance` then waits that long, and half a second more, between publishing and subscribing wherever it checks that an earlier event doesn't arrive. `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel` declares 16 seconds.
* **`SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` now reads change streams with the same cursor loop as `NativeMongoSubscriptionModel`, instead of Spring Data's `MessageListenerContainer`.** The loop is in the new `occurrent-subscription-mongodb-common-blocking-change-stream` module. See [section 19 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#19-springmongosubscriptionmodel-reads-its-own-cursor-and-a-quiet-durable-subscription-saves-its-position) for what changes with it, and [ADR 142](doc/architecture/decisions/0142-a-quiet-mongodb-subscription-moves-its-position-from-the-empty-batch.md).
  * An event whose action still throws after the `RetryStrategy` gave up is delivered again instead of skipped, so a `DurableSubscriptionModel` over it no longer stores a position past it.
  * A pause or a cancel before the change stream opens now stops it. After lost history with restarting turned off, the model forgets the subscription (fixes 0.7.4). The executor it makes by default is shut down in `shutdown()` (fixes 0.9.0).
* **`SpringMongoSubscriptionModel.start()` returns when the model gives up restarting a change stream, and pause, cancel and shutdown no longer wait for it.** Before, `start()` never returned after a `RetryStrategy` give-up or lost history with `restartSubscriptionsOnChangeStreamHistoryLost` turned off. A restart loop that was waiting when its subscription was paused now stops at its next attempt. Before, it could replace the change stream a resume had opened and leave a second loop parked forever, which shipped in 0.30.0. The subscription `resumeSubscription(..)` returns now follows a restarted change stream, so its `waitUntilStarted()` returns once the replacement opens.
* **A `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` built with `autoStartup(false)` no longer delivers every event twice to a subscription registered before `start()`.** `start()` opened that subscription's change stream twice. `start(false)` opened it too, so the subscription received events while `isPaused(..)` returned `true`, and `resumeSubscription(..)` for one subscription before `start()` opened the others as well. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.32.0.
* **`NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` knows a subscription as soon as `subscribe(..)` returns, so you can pause or cancel it while its change stream can't open.** Before, while MongoDB couldn't be reached, `isRunning(id)` returned `false`, `pauseSubscription(id)` threw `UnknownSubscriptionException` and `cancelSubscription(id)` did nothing, so the subscription started delivering once MongoDB was back. Behind a `CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel`, a node that lost its lease then went on consuming next to the new owner. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.7.0. Resolves [#1167](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1167).
  * A subscription made while the model is stopped opens no change stream until `start()` or `resumeSubscription(..)`. `start()` waits for MongoDB's operation time, and `start(false)` and `resumeSubscription(..)` don't. Before, `subscribe(..)` and `start()` each opened one, so every event arrived twice and neither a pause nor a cancel closed the first. See [section 16 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#16-a-native-mongodb-subscription-made-while-the-model-is-stopped-starts-with-start).
* **`NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` fixes for pausing, cancelling and `start()`, all shipped in 0.7.0 unless noted.**
  * A subscription paused or cancelled while it waits to restart a failed change stream stays that way, where the restart used to deliver to it again. For a `MongoException` or an `IllegalStateException` that shipped in 0.30.0.
  * An event the change stream had already fetched is delivered after the resume, not after `pauseSubscription(..)` has returned.
  * `start()` no longer holds the model's lock while it waits, which `subscriptionIds()` also waited for since 0.32.0. When one resume throws it still resumes the rest, which used to stay paused, and it returns when a change stream won't open, which waited forever since 0.30.0 for lost history. Its javadoc has the details.
* **An action that returns late no longer moves a `NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` subscription past the earlier position it was resumed at.** Before, when the resumed change stream hadn't opened yet, the position the action wrote replaced the one given to `resumeSubscription(id, startAt)`, and the events in between were never delivered again. `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` does the same now, and `DurableSubscriptionModel` no longer stores a checkpoint for an action that returns after `cancelSubscription(..)`, so a later subscribe of the id no longer starts after that event. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. See [ADR 142](doc/architecture/decisions/0142-a-quiet-mongodb-subscription-moves-its-position-from-the-empty-batch.md).
* **`NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` and `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` evaluate a `StartAt.dynamic(..)` position once each time they open a change stream.** The native model also evaluated it right after `subscribe(..)`, so through a `DurableSubscriptionModel` it read the checkpoint store twice before the change stream opened. See [ADR 142](doc/architecture/decisions/0142-a-quiet-mongodb-subscription-moves-its-position-from-the-empty-batch.md).
* **`NativeMongoSubscriptionModel.pauseSubscription(..)` and `stop()` no longer wait for a read that returns nothing.** Each pause used to wait up to a second for it, so stopping a model with many quiet subscriptions took up to a second per subscription. They still wait up to a second for an action that is running, except for a pause called from inside the action. When a fixed-size executor has no thread free for a resume, both MongoDB models now keep handing it the subscription, where the native model used to throw and leave it paused. See [section 19 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#a-pause-waits-for-an-action-that-is-running-and-the-model-checks-before-each-attempt) for how many threads that takes.
* **`NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` restarts a change stream whose cursor the driver reports as no longer open when the model didn't close it.** Before, the subscription stopped receiving events while `isRunning(id)` still returned `true`, and nothing above debug level was logged. See [ADR 142](doc/architecture/decisions/0142-a-quiet-mongodb-subscription-moves-its-position-from-the-empty-batch.md).
* **`waitUntilStarted(..)` on a `NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` or `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` subscription keeps the thread's interrupt flag when the thread is interrupted.** It still throws a `RuntimeException` caused by the `InterruptedException`, but it used to clear the flag, so code further up the call stack couldn't tell the thread had been asked to stop. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.1.0.
* **A durable MongoDB subscription that restarts after losing its history now stores where it restarts from first.** This is `DurableSubscriptionModel` over `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` or `NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` with `restartSubscriptionsOnChangeStreamHistoryLost` turned on. Before, the lost position stayed stored until the next event, so a process that stopped before then skipped everything written while it was down. When another node has written the checkpoint with a newer lease, the store is refused with a `WARN` and the subscription restarts anyway. See [the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#a-quiet-subscriptions-checkpoint-is-written-once-a-minute) and [ADR 142](doc/architecture/decisions/0142-a-quiet-mongodb-subscription-moves-its-position-from-the-empty-batch.md). Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.7.4 for `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` and 0.30.0 for `NativeMongoSubscriptionModel`.
* **`EveryN` now counts each subscription's events on its own.** That holds for `DurableSubscriptionModel`, `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` and the catch-up models, with one count for a whole catch-up however many windows it reads the history in. Before, all subscriptions of a model shared one count, so a subscription could go far longer than `n` events without a checkpoint and got all of them again after a restart. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.1.1.
* **Cancelling a subscription now cancels it fully in `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel`, `InMemorySubscriptionModel` and `ManualStartSubscriptionModel`.**
  * `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel.cancelSubscription(..)` forgets a paused subscription, so a competing consumer model over it forgets one this node holds no lease for too. Before, its id couldn't be subscribed again, `start(true)` delivered to it, and one made while the model was stopped opened its change stream on `start()`. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.7.0.
  * `InMemorySubscriptionModel.cancelSubscription(..)` stops the subscription's thread, so its queued events no longer reach the action after the cancel. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.5.0.
  * `ManualStartSubscriptionModel.cancelSubscription(..)` while `subscribe(..)` or `resumeSubscription(..)` is starting the same subscription now cancels it in the model it wraps too. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.32.0.
* **Cancelling a reactor durable subscription no longer keeps its old position stored, or deletes the position of the next subscription with that id.** `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel.cancelSubscription(..)` deletes the position only after the cancelled subscription's own position writes have ended, and retries a delete that fails. A `subscribe(..)` of the id in the same process before then puts the old position back, and one from `StartAt.subscriptionModelDefault()` resumes from it. To start the id clean, wait for the `Mono` the cancel now returns. Until it completes, a `subscribe(..)` of the id can throw `DuplicateSubscriptionIdException`. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.30.0. See sections [21](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call), [23](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#23-a-reactor-cancelsubscription-returns-a-mono-that-completes-once-the-stored-state-is-deleted) and [25](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#25-a-durable-reactor-subscribe-from-the-model-default-returns-without-waiting-for-storage) of the upgrade guide.
* **The reactor `CheckpointStorage` gains `delete(subscriptionId, condition)` and `evaluatesDeleteConditions()`.** The in-memory and MongoDB reactor storages implement both, so a cancel's delete and the next subscription's reads and writes don't wait for each other. A storage of your own keeps compiling. On it, a subscription of the id made during the cancel's delete writes no position until the delete attempt under way has ended. Implement both methods to remove that wait. See [section 23](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#23-a-reactor-cancelsubscription-returns-a-mono-that-completes-once-the-stored-state-is-deleted) of the upgrade guide.
* **A reactor durable subscription that starts at the present no longer misses events written just after `subscribe(..)` returns.** This applies to `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` over a model of your own that is not a `SubscriptionModel`, from `StartAt.now()`, or from `StartAt.subscriptionModelDefault()` with nothing stored. It now asks that model's `globalCheckpointAsOfNow()` at the call, and no call waits for the answer. A read that never answers keeps the subscription from starting, with a warning every 10 seconds. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. See [section 21](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call) and [section 23](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#23-a-reactor-cancelsubscription-returns-a-mono-that-completes-once-the-stored-state-is-deleted) of the upgrade guide.
* **`ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` no longer stores a checkpoint for a `subscribe(..)` that the model it wraps refuses.** This applies over a model that manages named subscriptions, such as `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel`. The leftover checkpoint could make a restart resume from a position the running subscription may not have reached. A `subscribe(..)` of an id that model reports as running or paused now throws `DuplicateSubscriptionIdException` at the call, without calling your `StartAt.dynamic(..)` function. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. Part of [#1197](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1197). See [section 25](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#25-a-durable-reactor-subscribe-from-the-model-default-returns-without-waiting-for-storage) of the upgrade guide.
* **A slow storage read, `StartAt.dynamic(..)` function or feed cancel for one id no longer holds up `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` calls for other ids.** This applies over a model that is not a `SubscriptionModel`. A cancel, pause, `stop()` or `shutdown()` before a subscription has started now ends it, and its `waitUntilStarted()` fails with `CancellationException`, or `SubscriptionModelShutdownException` after `shutdown()`, where 0.33.0 never ended that wait. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. See section [23](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#23-a-reactor-cancelsubscription-returns-a-mono-that-completes-once-the-stored-state-is-deleted) of the upgrade guide.
  * `start(true)` now keeps only a subscription whose dynamic start position threw paused, starts the others, and throws the first error with later ones suppressed. It still starts them one by one on the calling thread.
* **`ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` no longer refuses every later `subscribe(..)` of an id as a duplicate after a dynamic `StartAt` threw for that id.** This applies over a model that is not a `SubscriptionModel`. A throw, a failed storage read for example, left the id registered with nothing running under it. A `resumeSubscription(..)` that throws the same way now keeps the subscription paused, where before it was reported as running. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0.
* **`ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` no longer loses track of a subscription registered again while an earlier failed attempt with the same id is still finishing.** Registering again is the documented way to recover from a refused registration. The first attempt's failure could remove the second registration, which then kept running but was unknown to `isRunning`, `isPaused`, `subscriptionIds` and `cancelSubscription`. See [#791](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/791).
* **Subscribing with an id to a `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` or a reactor catch-up model after `shutdown()` now throws before any history is replayed.** `subscribe(..)` throws `SubscriptionModelShutdownException`. Before, on the model the reactive MongoDB starter builds, a subscription from the beginning or an explicit position replayed history into its handler and failed only when it switched to live events. A subscribe that runs at the same time as the shutdown either throws or is cancelled by it. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0.
* **`waitUntilStarted()` on a reactor catch-up subscription now fails with `SubscriptionModelShutdownException` when the model shuts down before the subscription switched to live events.** Before, it never completed. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0.
* **`ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` logs a failed position read at registration at `WARN` instead of `ERROR`.** A stored checkpoint, or the subscription's own start position, can still let it start. The refusal message for a position source that answers nothing, here and in `ManualStartSubscriptionModel`, no longer claims there is no position when a stored checkpoint settles the registration, and no longer says "while registering" for a read made when a stopped subscription starts. See [#804](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/804) and [#805](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/805).

##### Catch-up subscriptions

* **A position catch-up restarted in the middle of its replay no longer misses an event that committed late.** Every checkpoint stored during the replay now also holds the change-stream position live delivery starts from, read before the replay. This covers the stream and DCB catch-ups on both stacks, the reactor ones under `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel`. Some events can now arrive twice, and a catch-up whose stored position has left the oplog 4 replays in a row fails with an `IllegalStateException`. When the oplog drops the live start after the last check and before the change stream opens, the MongoDB subscription models now ask the catch-up first, and it replays again instead of the model going live from the present and skipping the events in between. That holds for the time-based catch-up too. A subscription model of your own can ask the same way through the new `HistoryLossHandling` answer of `HistoryLossListener.handleHistoryLoss(..)`, and the reactor API now has `HistoryLossReportingSubscriptions` and `RepositionableSubscriptions` as well, which `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` passes on from the model it wraps. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. See [ADR 144](doc/architecture/decisions/0144-a-catch-up-checkpoint-keeps-the-live-start-it-read-before-the-replay.md), [ADR 146](doc/architecture/decisions/0146-a-catch-up-takes-over-a-live-start-the-wrapped-model-loses-as-it-opens.md) and [section 26 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#26-a-position-catch-up-stores-the-live-start-it-read-before-the-replay).
* **A time-based catch-up no longer misses an event with an earlier time that commits during its replay, or while it is stopped mid-replay.** The blocking `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` now reads where live delivery starts before a time replay, and stores it in the new `CatchupTimeCheckpoint`. More events can arrive twice, also without a restart, and a long time replay now depends on the oplog. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.18.0 for a single run and in 0.2.0 for a restart. See [ADR 144](doc/architecture/decisions/0144-a-catch-up-checkpoint-keeps-the-live-start-it-read-before-the-replay.md) and [section 28 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#28-a-time-based-catch-up-reads-its-live-start-before-the-replay).
* **A blocking catch-up from `StartAtTime.offsetDateTime(..)` on an event store that writes positions now resumes from the time it stored.** This covers `CatchupSubscriptionModel` and `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel`, which now store the time of the events the replay handles on such a store too. Before, a restart with `StartAt.subscriptionModelDefault()` went straight to live delivery and never delivered the rest of the history. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.30.0.
* **A blocking catch-up whose `StartAt.dynamic(..)` answers `StartAt.subscriptionModelDefault()` now resumes a replay that stopped in the middle.** That is the start the Spring Boot starter, `ResumeStartPositions.replayThenResume(..)` and `replayThenResumeDcb(..)` use for a subscription that replays from the beginning or a given position and then resumes the default way. Before, `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` and `DcbCatchupSubscriptionModel` looked for a stored checkpoint only when the `StartAt` was the default itself, so a restart mid-replay went live and skipped the rest of the history. The reactor catch-ups already resumed. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0.
* **A blocking catch-up runs a replay that `stop()` cut short again on `start(true)` or `resumeSubscription(id)`, instead of ending it for good.** This covers `CatchupSubscriptionModel` and `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel`, and a subscription made while the model is stopped too. Until then the subscription counts as paused. The replay runs again from the last position it stored, so some events arrive twice. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.30.0 for `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` and in 0.32.0 for `CatchupSubscriptionModel`. See [ADR 141](doc/architecture/decisions/0141-a-stopped-subscription-model-holds-a-new-subscription-paused-until-it-is-started.md).
  * A shutdown while `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` or `DcbCatchupSubscriptionModel` replays, before it has stored a position, no longer stores the present as the subscription's position. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.18.0 for `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel`, then under another name, and in 0.30.0 for `DcbCatchupSubscriptionModel`.
* **A blocking catch-up's replay that runs again after a stop never runs your action twice at once.** It waits until your action returns from an event of the cut-short replay. `start(..)` and `resumeSubscription(..)` return without waiting, and a cancel or another stop ends the wait until the next start or resume.
  * A `start(false)` while the cut-short replay is still being set aside does run it, so its events can reach your action without a resume, some of them twice.
  * On a stopped model, `resumeSubscription(id)` calls `start(false)` first also for a subscription that was live before the stop, and a subscription made after the resume replays straight away.
  * When `start(..)` throws, a stopped model stays stopped unless the model it wraps runs afterwards or another start or resume came meanwhile. `resumeSubscription(id, startAt)` on `CatchupSubscriptionModel` then calls no `start(false)`, but once the model it wraps returns from it, a subscription made afterwards replays straight away.
* **A reactor catch-up runs a replay that `stop()` cut short again only on `start(true)` or `resumeSubscription(..)`.** This covers `ReactorCatchupSubscriptionModel`, `ReactorStreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` and `ReactorDcbCatchupSubscriptionModel`, and a subscription made while the model is stopped. Until then the subscription counts as paused, `isRunning(..)` returns `false`, and `pauseSubscription(..)` throws `SubscriptionNotRunningException`. Before, `start(false)` ran the replay without a resume, and `isPaused(..)` said `true` only after a `pauseSubscription(..)`. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.32.0. Resolves [#1212](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1212).
  * On a stopped model, `resumeSubscription(..)` calls `start(false)` first and resumes nothing else, also for a subscription already live. A `ReactorCatchupSubscriptionModel` starts every catch-up model it routes to. When that start throws, nothing is resumed and the next resume tries again. Before, the resume left the model stopped, could throw `SubscriptionAlreadyRunningException`, and a subscription made after it replayed only on the next `start(..)`.
  * A cut-short replay no longer goes live without its remaining history when `start(..)` comes early. `isRunning()` on a stopped model returns `false` while `start(..)` waits for the model underneath, and after that start throws.
* **A cancel that races a blocking catch-up's switch from replay to live events is no longer lost.** A stale attempt's late cleanup can no longer wipe a newer attempt's saved position for the same id either. `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` and the DCB catch-up model now hold a lock per subscription id for the whole switch. See [ADR 131](doc/architecture/decisions/0131-a-per-id-lock-closes-the-blocking-catchup-handover-race.md). Fixes [#827](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/827).
* **A reactor catch-up now delivers an event twice when it was written while the replay ran.** Make your reactor handler safe to run twice, for example by keying the work on the CloudEvent id. `handoverCacheSize` now sizes only the overlap between the replay and live delivery. This changes behaviour the reactive catch-up models shipped in 0.30.0. Resolves [#891](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/891). See [ADR 135](doc/architecture/decisions/0135-the-reactive-handover-dedup-is-fed-only-by-the-reconciliation-read.md) and [section 9 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#9-a-reactor-catch-up-subscription-can-deliver-a-concurrent-write-twice).
* **A catch-up no longer drops a live event that shares only its id with a replayed event from another producer.** CloudEvents makes only `id` and `source` together unique, and `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel`, `DcbCatchupSubscriptionModel` and their reactor counterparts now compare both. No public API changes, and since this only delivers events that were dropped before, there is nothing to migrate. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. Resolves [#1048](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1048).
* **A catch-up that stops on a checked exception from a replayed handler now records the failure.** A Kotlin handler can throw one without declaring it. The blocking catch-up then went on buffering live events, and `accept(...)` reported each one as handled until the buffer was full, 100,000 events by default. A caller that waited for the catch-up already got the exception. Now the catch-up refuses every later live event, and nothing needs to change on your side. This covers `CatchupThenPushSubscriptionModel`, the blocking `CatchupProjectionFeed` and `DomainEventFeed`, and `@Projection` and `@Saga` with `source = PUSH`. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. Resolves [#1093](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1093).
  * `CatchupThenPushSubscriptionModel.isCatchingUp(id)` answers `false` after such a failure and logs it. Under `startupMode = BACKGROUND`, a `@Projection(source = PUSH)` fed by a `DomainEventFeed` logs it and reports it as failed in `PushCatchupStatus`.
  * A `ReplayAware` view whose `replayAbandoned()` throws a checked exception no longer hides a failed replay. The blocking stack lost the failure and the reactor catch-up never completed.
* **A blocking catch-up that fails on an interrupted thread no longer makes its `CatchupProjectionFeed`, `DomainEventFeed` or `CatchupThenPushSubscriptionModel` refuse every later event.** Only the interrupt flag counts, so an `InterruptedIOException` on a thread that isn't interrupted still fails the catch-up. A feed still throws the failure, and the thread stays interrupted. `CatchupThenPushSubscriptionModel` replays again on the same thread, and gives up after 3 retries in a row that get no further into the history. A Spring Boot projection with `startupMode = BACKGROUND` on a `DomainEventFeed` is caught up again after 100, 200 and 400 ms, and logs an error when the third retry fails too. While no replay runs, an event from the in-memory event store's write path is dropped, and the next replay reads it from the store.
* **A blocking `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` or `DcbCatchupSubscriptionModel` whose replay fails now logs the failure at `ERROR`.** Before, only a caller of `waitUntilStarted()` learned about it, and the Spring Boot starter waits only when `startupMode` says so. A failed event store read or a function that threw during the replay then left the subscription receiving nothing, with nothing in the log. A catch-up that was cancelled, stopped, shut down or replaced by a newer subscribe for the same id isn't logged. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0.
* **A reactor catch-up whose replayed fold or handler throws a JVM-fatal error now fails instead of running forever.** This covers the reactor `CatchupProjectionFeed`, `DomainEventFeed` and `CatchupThenPushSubscriptionModel`, and a `StackOverflowError`, `NoClassDefFoundError` or `ExceptionInInitializerError` that a fold the catch-up runs throws while it is called or subscribed, before its `Mono` has ended. That is the fold of a replayed event, or of an event fed by a replayed fold, `replayStarted()` or `replayCompleted()`. The catch-up writes no marker, `catchUp()`, `catchUpAll()` and `waitUntilStarted()` error with the thrown error, and the error the catch-up logs includes it. Reactor rethrows such an error instead of passing it on, so it used to reach `Schedulers.onHandleError` and leave the catch-up running with nothing answered. An error a live fold throws still goes to Reactor's own handling, and so does one a fold's `Mono` throws after it has ended or on a scheduler thread it switched to itself. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0.
* **A reactor catch-up now drops a live event it buffered when its replay is stopped before going live.** This covers the reactor `CatchupThenPushSubscriptionModel` and `CatchupProjectionFeed`, where 0.33.0 applied it once the next catch-up went live. The `Mono` from `CatchupProjectionFeed.acceptAndWait(..)`, `accept(..)` in 0.33.0, now errors with an `IllegalStateException`, so a listener that acknowledges on completion does not acknowledge it, and has the broker deliver it again. Have the broker deliver it again for an event that `acceptOrDefer(..)` or `DomainEventFeed.acceptOrDeferCloudEvent(..)` reports as `RoutingOutcome.DEFERRED` too. Leaving the message unacknowledged is not enough on its own, and the javadoc of `RoutingOutcome.Disposition.HOLD` says what RabbitMQ and Kafka need instead. Resolves [#1130](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1130).
  * A dropped event frees its place in the live buffer. In 0.33.0 repeated stops could fill the buffer, and every new event was refused until a catch-up went live.
* **A blocking subscription that resumes from the position another node's catch-up stored during its replay no longer skips events.** `CatchupSubscriptionModel` and `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` now replay from that position on `resumeSubscription(id)` or `start(true)`, so events the other node handled arrive again. The catch-up model runs one call at a time for each subscription id, and `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` and `DcbCatchupSubscriptionModel` now implement `RepositionableSubscriptions`. A pause through `CatchupSubscriptionModel` during the replay a subscribe started now reaches that replay, where 0.33.0 sent it to the wrapped model, which doesn't have the subscription until the replay hands over. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. See [section 29 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#29-a-blocking-subscription-that-resumes-from-another-nodes-catch-up-position-replays-from-it). Resolves [#1219](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1219).

##### Competing consumers

* **A competing consumer node holds each event back while its lease may have expired.** The event waits while the lease strategy's `hasLock(..)` answers `false` or throws. Some calls into the model it wraps let it through without the lease, so it can reach two nodes, but it's never skipped or lost. The MongoDB lease strategies answer `false` from three quarters of the lease time after the request that last set the lease was sent, 15 seconds by default. Resolves [#1176](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1176). Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.9.0.
  * An underlying model of your own should not hold a lock while it hands an event over, and a `CompetingConsumerStrategy` of your own should answer `hasLock(..)` from memory, since it's called for every event. Their javadoc lists the rest ([model](subscription/util/blocking/competing-consumer-subscription/src/main/java/org/occurrent/subscription/blocking/competingconsumers/CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel.java), [strategy](subscription/api/blocking/src/main/java/org/occurrent/subscription/api/blocking/CompetingConsumerStrategy.java)).
* **A competing subscription recovers by itself when MongoDB or the model it wraps fails.** A failed call to the lease strategy or the model it wraps is tried again on a thread of its own, with a backoff, until it succeeds. A node that fails to start or resume a subscription gives the lease back, so another node can take over. `start(..)` and `resumeSubscription(..)` log such a failure and return, see [section 18 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#18-a-competing-consumers-start-and-resumesubscription-log-a-failure-and-return). `start()` gives every subscription its turn before it throws the first failure of one that doesn't compete, with the others attached as suppressed. Resolves [#1166](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1166). Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.9.0, 0.19.0 and 0.32.0.
* **`CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel.stop()` now stops the model and gives up its leases whatever state the model it wraps is in.** Before, `stop()` did nothing while the model it wraps wasn't running, so a subscription waiting for its lease started once this node won it. `stop()` now pauses each subscription still running in the model it wraps before it gives up that lease. When the underlying model's own `stop()` throws, `stop()` throws an `IllegalStateException` naming the subscriptions it paused, with any it could not pause attached as suppressed. A subscription you resume after `stop()` runs once this node wins its lease, instead of staying paused for good. See [ADR 141](doc/architecture/decisions/0141-a-stopped-subscription-model-holds-a-new-subscription-paused-until-it-is-started.md). Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.9.0.
* **Winning a lease no longer resumes subscriptions whose lease another node holds.** Before, a node whose underlying model wasn't running resumed every subscription that model held paused, so two nodes handled the same events. Pausing a subscription that waits for its lease is now recorded, where it threw `SubscriptionNotRunningException`. Both fix behaviour shipped in 0.9.0.
  * `start(false)` makes a subscription that was waiting for its lease, or had lost it, compete again, and a lease won while the underlying model is stopped now starts that model. Before, the subscription stayed paused. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.32.0.
* **A subscription made on a stopped competing consumer model no longer takes a lease before `start()`.** Before, it could win the lease and keep it until the next `start()`, locking every other node out of a subscription this node didn't serve. The model it wraps now gets the subscription straight away and holds it paused, through the new `SubscriptionModel.subscribePaused(..)`. An underlying model of your own that doesn't implement it gets the subscription as in 0.33.0 and can deliver before `start()`. A `subscribe(..)` after `shutdown()` throws `IllegalStateException`. See [ADR 141](doc/architecture/decisions/0141-a-stopped-subscription-model-holds-a-new-subscription-paused-until-it-is-started.md), which lists the models that implement `subscribePaused(..)`. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.9.0.
* **A handler still running after its node lost the lease can no longer move the checkpoint back.** It now writes its checkpoint with the fencing token of the lease it ran under, a number that grows each time the lease changes owner, so the write is refused once the next holder has written. Before, the next resume delivered again what the new holder had already handled. After such a refusal `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` stops handing that subscription's events to the handler, as `NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` already did. See [ADR 139](doc/architecture/decisions/0139-a-node-that-gave-up-a-lease-writes-with-the-token-it-held.md). Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0.
* **One lease that is slow or refused to refresh no longer lets the node's other leases expire.** A lease MongoDB refuses to refresh no longer skips the rest of that refresh round. The MongoDB lease strategies call a `CompetingConsumerListener` from a thread of its own, so a pause waiting for a slow change stream no longer stops the node refreshing its other leases. See [ADR 140](doc/architecture/decisions/0140-a-refresh-round-tells-its-listeners-on-a-thread-of-its-own.md). Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.9.0.
* **A MongoDB outage on one competing subscription no longer holds up the others on the node, or `shutdown()`.** A call that waits for the lease strategy or the underlying model now holds up only the calls for its own subscription, unless a `stop()` or `shutdown()` waits for it. `shutdown()` makes one attempt to give up each lease and waits at most five seconds. A lease it doesn't give up expires after the lease time, 20 seconds by default. When the underlying model throws from its own `shutdown()`, `shutdown()` gives up no lease and throws, see [section 24 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#24-a-competing-consumer-whose-wrapped-model-fails-to-shut-down-lets-its-leases-expire). See also [ADR 140](doc/architecture/decisions/0140-a-refresh-round-tells-its-listeners-on-a-thread-of-its-own.md) and [ADR 141](doc/architecture/decisions/0141-a-stopped-subscription-model-holds-a-new-subscription-paused-until-it-is-started.md). Resolves [#1175](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1175). Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.9.0.
* **`CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel.isRunning()` now says whether the competing consumer model is started, not what the model it wraps says.** It returns `true` after `start(..)`, also while this node holds no lease, and `false` after `stop()` and once `shutdown()` has begun. To find out whether a subscription runs on this node, call `isRunning(id)`. `isRunning()` no longer calls the underlying model, so it doesn't wait for a lock that model holds while it hands an event over. See [section 20 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#20-a-competing-consumers-isrunning-says-whether-the-model-is-started). Resolves [#1177](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1177). Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.9.0.
* **A competing consumer model over a model that isn't running stays stopped until its own `start(..)`, and every `start(..)` starts the model it wraps.** Over a `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` built with `autoStartup(false)`, nothing is delivered before that `start(..)` unless you start the `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` yourself. A competing subscription you paused directly on the underlying model runs again on the next `start(..)`, lease grant or resume, so pause it through the competing consumer model instead. See [section 22](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#22-a-competing-consumer-over-a-wrapped-model-that-is-not-running-waits-for-its-own-start) and [Pause a competing subscription through the competing consumer model](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#pause-a-competing-subscription-through-the-competing-consumer-model). Resolves [#1186](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1186).
  * When a `start(true)` comes while `subscribe(..)` makes a subscription that doesn't compete, and resuming it fails, `subscribe(..)` throws. The subscription exists by then, so resume or cancel it as usual.
* **`start(false)` on a competing consumer model no longer resumes a paused subscription that doesn't compete for a lease.** It stays paused until `resumeSubscription(..)` or `start(true)`, as `start(false)` is documented to do. Over an underlying model that isn't running, each `start(..)` still resumes such subscriptions, except one you paused, until a `start(..)` returns without throwing. See item 4 of [section 18 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#18-a-competing-consumers-start-and-resumesubscription-log-a-failure-and-return). Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.19.0.
* **Four fixes to cancel, resubscribe and pause in the blocking competing consumer, durable and catch-up models.** `CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel` gives up the lease when the model it wraps refuses to pause, so another node can take over from a node whose catch-up already failed. `DurableSubscriptionModel` resumes from its checkpoint again for a subscription that opts out once and later subscribes again as managed. In `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` and the DCB catch-up model, cancelling and resubscribing right away no longer lets the cancelled replay go live, makes the new subscription look cancelled, or lets an event still being handled write back the checkpoint the cancel deleted. Resolves [#737](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/737).
* **Closing one subscription while MongoDB is unreachable no longer hangs.** `NativeMongoLeaseCompetingConsumerStrategy` and `SpringMongoLeaseCompetingConsumerStrategy` retried removing a lease forever, so `close()` on a saga or competing consumer subscription, and every shutdown step after it, waited for the database. Unregistering and releasing now make at most 5 attempts, or fewer if configured, and the lease then expires so any node can take over. Registering is unchanged. Resolves [#999](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/999).
  * A refresh round no longer raises a limit below 5 to 5. Shutting the strategy down stops a call mid-backoff. A saga subscription's `close()` survives a checked exception from the lease release, which a `RetryStrategy` with `mapError` can throw and which used to skip the timer poller shutdown.

##### Other changes

* **`Projection.builder()`, `Projection.singletonBuilder()`, `Saga.builder()`, `SnapshotView.builder()`, `View.create(BiFunction)` and `View.create(Fold)` can now be called without an initial state.** Use that instead of passing `null` when the first state comes from the first event, as `FlowSaga.builder()` already allowed. The state is then `@Nullable S`, which Kotlin reads as `S?`, so in Kotlin a handler that uses it without a `null` check, or a variable typed `View<String, String>`, fails to compile instead of throwing on the first event. Kotlin's `projection { }`, `singletonProjection { }`, `saga { }`, `snapshotView { }` and `view { }` get the same form. Resolves [#956](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/956).
* **`RetryStrategy.execute(..)` takes a shutdown predicate, so a component that is shutting down can stop a retry loop that is already running.** Pass your lifecycle flag, `retryStrategy.execute(runnable, __ -> running)` for example. The loop rethrows the last failure once the predicate answers `false`, which it checks before every retry and about every 50 ms during a backoff. An interrupted backoff still restores the interrupt flag. Use this rather than a flag in `retryIf(..)`, which is read only between attempts and replaces your own predicate. A `RetryStrategy` you implement yourself ignores the predicate, and the MongoDB competing consumer strategies and `InMemoryDeadlineConsumerRegistry` warn at startup when given one. Resolves [#866](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/866).
* **`InMemoryDeadlineConsumerRegistry` keeps the retry predicate you configure, and `shutdown()` no longer waits out a backoff.** The registry put its own running flag in `retryIf(..)`, which replaced the predicate in your `Config.retryStrategy(RetryStrategy.retry().retryIf(..))`, so exceptions you had excluded were retried. It now uses the shutdown predicate above, and `shutdown()` stops a consumer between retries instead of waiting out its backoff, one second with the default strategy. A consumer failure the strategy gives up on is now logged and the registry takes the next deadline, where it used to end the one polling thread that serves every category. Resolves [#999](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/999).
* **A snapshot decider command no longer fails after its events are written when your `SnapshotStore` or `SnapshotPolicy` throws a checked exception or an `Error`.** A caller seeing that failure was likely to run the command again. `SnapshotDeciderApplicationService` and `SnapshotDcbDeciderApplicationService` now log it and skip the snapshot, which the next replay rebuilds, and the same goes for deleting a snapshot ahead of a stream you reset. A blocking service that catches an `InterruptedException` this way sets the interrupt flag again. The reactive services do the same when your `ReactiveSnapshotStore` or `SnapshotPolicy` throws before returning a `Mono`, but a `StackOverflowError` or `LinkageError` raised while that `Mono` runs still means the command's `Mono` never completes. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. Resolves [#1097](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1097).
* **`executeAndReturnState` on `DcbDeciderApplicationService`, `ReactiveSnapshotDeciderApplicationService` and `ReactiveSnapshotDcbDeciderApplicationService` refuses a `null` state before writing the command's events.** Before, a decider whose decided state was `null` had its events written and then threw a `NullPointerException`, and a caller seeing the failure was likely to run the command again. `executeAndReturnDecision` still returns a decision whose state can be `null`. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. Resolves [#1103](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1103) and [#1114](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1114).
* **A checked exception from one `SynchronousSubscriptionModel` handler outside a transaction no longer keeps the other handlers from getting the events just written.** They all receive every event, and the exception reaches the caller afterwards, the way a `RuntimeException` already did. The model has no replay, so those handlers never saw the events, and a handler written in Kotlin can throw a checked exception without declaring it. An `Error` still stops the dispatch at once. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.33.0. Resolves [#1102](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1102).

#### Breaking changes

* **The flow saga's `join`, Kotlin's `expect<T>` and `Expectation` are removed.** Use `on(allOf(...))` instead. `UpgradeToOccurrent_0_34` rewrites a Java `join` whose expectations are a literal list, and the compiler finds the rest, every Kotlin call included. See [section 1 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#1-a-flow-sagas-join-kotlins-expectt-and-expectation-are-removed), [ADR 125](doc/architecture/decisions/0125-a-lowered-joins-reaction-reads-its-own-window-not-the-whole-retained-history.md), [#707](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/707) and [#806](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/806).
* **`SagaEnvelope`, `SagaInstance`, `SagaStatus` and `SagaRunnerConfig` each gain a member for saga quarantine.** A record pattern over `SagaEnvelope` or `SagaRunnerConfig`, an exhaustive `switch` or `when` over `SagaStatus`, and your own `SagaInstance` stop compiling. Once quarantine is on, `findByStatus(ACTIVE, ..)` no longer returns a quarantined instance. There is no recipe. See [section 8 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#8-a-saga-instance-that-keeps-failing-can-be-quarantined-and-four-saga-types-change-with-it).
* **More places refuse a declared event type whose concrete subtypes can't be found.** That covers a projection, a subscription DSL subscription, a `DomainEventQueries` query, a `@Snapshot` view, `ExecuteFilter`'s `type` and `includeTypes`, and `DcbCriteriaBuilder`'s `type` and `types`. Each of them, a saga and an annotation-based subscription too, now also refuses a concrete class that is neither final nor sealed. Mark the class `final`, seal the hierarchy, declare the concrete types or set an explicit filter. There is no recipe. See [section 3 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#3-declaring-an-event-type-whose-concrete-subtypes-cannot-be-found-is-refused), [ADR 126](doc/architecture/decisions/0126-every-derived-event-type-filter-expands-a-declared-sealed-type.md), [#753](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/753) and [#912](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/912).
* **`ExecuteFilter.excludeTypes` refuses an array or a primitive declared type with `IllegalArgumentException`.** Declare the concrete event types you meant, or build the filter with `ExecuteFilter.from(StreamReadFilter)` to exclude an array type. See [section 3 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#3-declaring-an-event-type-whose-concrete-subtypes-cannot-be-found-is-refused) and [#912](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/912).
* **`WriteResult` and `DcbAppendResult` gain a fourth component, `Optional<AppendId>`.** An `isEqualTo(...)` on a whole result now fails, so compare `streamId`, `oldStreamVersion` and `newStreamVersion`, or the DCB equivalents. A record pattern naming three components stops compiling, and `UpgradeToOccurrent_0_34` adds `var appendId` to it. See [section 6 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#6-writeresult-and-dcbappendresult-gain-a-fourth-component-the-append-id), [ADR 132](doc/architecture/decisions/0132-an-append-has-an-identity-and-read-your-writes-becomes-a-membership-question.md) and [#740](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/740).
* **A MongoDB event store with `DCB` and without `STREAM` no longer creates `dcbTags_1`.** Enabling `STREAM` on such a store builds the index at startup when the collection doesn't have it, so on a large collection build it yourself first. See [section 27 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#27-a-dcb-only-mongodb-event-store-no-longer-creates-the-dcbtags-index).
* **Four MongoDB-only configuration keys move under `mongodb`, such as `occurrent.event-store.collection` to `occurrent.event-store.mongodb.collection`.** The old keys still work until the release after next, and `UpgradeToOccurrent_0_34` rewrites `.properties` and `.yaml` files. See [section 4 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#4-four-mongodb-only-keys-move-under-mongodb) and [#439](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/439).
* **Class-level advice on a `@Projection`, `@Saga` or `@Snapshot` bean, `@Transactional` for example, no longer runs at startup when Spring calls its factory method.** A reactor factory method that returns `null` now fails startup with `IllegalStateException` rather than `IllegalArgumentException`, so a `catch` scoped to the latter no longer catches it. See [section 5 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#5-a-descriptor-factorys-class-level-advice-no-longer-runs-at-startup) and [its part on a null-returning factory](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#a-null-returning-factory-now-fails-differently-on-reactor).
* **A `@Subscription`, `@StreamSubscription`, `@DcbSubscription` or `@SynchronousSubscription` handler that Spring's proxy can't invoke is now refused with `SubscriptionHandlerNotInvocableException`.** Before, it ran on the raw bean with `@Transactional` and every other aspect skipped. The refusal fails startup, or for a bean built after startup, a `@Lazy` bean for example, that bean's creation or the first delivery to the handler. A live-only subscription also no longer sees an event a bean writes while singletons are created, from `@PostConstruct` for example. See [section 11 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#11-a-subscription-handler-spring-cannot-invoke-is-now-refused-and-a-live-subscription-can-miss-a-startup-write).
* **A projection feed's `accept(..)` no longer reports an event as handled when it wasn't applied, and is deprecated in favour of `acceptAndWait(..)`.** The blocking `CatchupProjectionFeed.acceptAndWait(..)` and `DomainEventFeed.acceptAndWait(..)` wait until the event is applied and throw `IllegalStateException` when it isn't. On the reactor stack their `Mono` errors for an event fed while the feed is stopped. Don't acknowledge the message then, and have the broker deliver it again. Never call the blocking `acceptAndWait(..)` on the thread that later runs the catch-up. The deprecated `accept(..)` calls `acceptAndWait(..)`, and `UpgradeToOccurrent_0_34` renames each call. The new `acceptOrDefer(..)` on both feeds never waits for a catch-up. It returns `RoutingOutcome.DELIVERED` once the event is applied, or `DEFERRED` before the feed is live. On the reactor stack `DELIVERED` also waits for the events the fold fed while applying it, and `DEFERRED` also comes back when a replay holds one of those back. There its `Mono` errors with an `IllegalStateException` when the feed's own code calls it, the fold or a callback such as `replayStarted()`, since the event cannot be applied before that code returns. A broker listener has the broker deliver a `DEFERRED` message again, as the javadoc of `RoutingOutcome.Disposition.HOLD` describes. See [section 12 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#12-a-projection-feeds-accept-waits-until-the-event-is-applied-and-fails-when-it-is-not), [#1135](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1135) and [#1209](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1209).
* **A `PushSubscriptionModel`'s `accept(..)` is supported only from the in-memory event store's write path.** With MongoDB or another durable store, use a durable subscription, or forward the events to a broker whose listener calls `acceptOrDefer(..)`. An HTTP endpoint whose caller does not retry, and a Spring application event that no event store holds, have no supported replacement. See [section 13 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#13-only-the-in-memory-event-stores-write-path-may-feed-a-push-models-accept), [#1140](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1140) and the amendment to [ADR 133](doc/architecture/decisions/0133-a-broker-is-a-transport-for-the-push-feed-and-never-a-subscription-model.md).
* **`CatchupProjectionFeed.goLive()` and `DomainEventFeed.goLive(id)` wait for a running catch-up of the same projection, on both stacks, and fail with `IllegalStateException` when it fails.** A call the view makes while the feed is calling it doesn't wait. See [section 14 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#14-a-projection-feeds-golive-waits-for-a-running-catch-up) for when a `goLive()` next to a catch-up is still needed.
* **A `catchUp()`, `catchUp(id)` or `catchUpAll()` that a view's own code calls on its blocking `CatchupProjectionFeed` or `DomainEventFeed` returns without waiting, and the replay it asks for starts only after that code returns.** 0.33.0 ran it inside that code. A view that hands the call to another thread and waits for it there waits for a replay that can't start. The javadoc of `CatchupProjectionFeed.catchUp()` covers failures and interrupts, and when the replay does not run at all. See [section 30 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#30-a-catchup-called-from-a-views-own-code-returns-without-waiting-for-the-replay) and [#1154](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1154).
* **Feeding an event back into a reactor `CatchupThenPushSubscriptionModel`, `CatchupProjectionFeed` or `DomainEventFeed` from its own handler or fold, or from a callback such as `replayStarted()`, no longer waits forever.** `accept(..)`, `acceptAndWait(..)` and `acceptAndWaitCloudEvent(..)` complete once the event is queued. When applying it fails, the failure goes to whatever waits for the event the handler or fold was called for. A listener's call errors. A replay's catch-up applies the event before it writes its marker and fails instead of writing it, so `catchUp()`, `catchUpAll()` and `waitUntilStarted()` error, and a `replayCompleted()` whose fed event fails fails the catch-up too. A failed catch-up makes the subscription or feed fail for good, and so does a failure to apply an event nothing waits for, one fed from `replayAbandoned()` say. Cancel and subscribe again, or build a new feed. A `catchUp()` that code calls on its own feed completes without waiting, where 0.33.0 failed the feed for good. See [section 15 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#15-feeding-a-reactor-catch-up-from-its-own-handler-no-longer-waits-forever), [#1148](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1148), [#1151](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1151) and [#1153](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1153).
* **A `DurableSubscriptionModel` subscription on a shared MongoDB Atlas cluster, or over any model whose `globalCheckpoint()` answers `null`, refuses to start unless a checkpoint is already stored.** Set `occurrent.subscription.start-when-no-start-position-can-be-recorded=true` or `DurableSubscriptionModelConfig.startWhenNoStartPositionCanBeRecorded(true)` to keep the 0.33.0 behaviour, or subscribe with a `StartAt` of your own. See [section 7 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#7-durablesubscriptionmodel-refuses-a-first-subscription-when-no-start-position-can-be-recorded).
* **A `NativeMongoSubscriptionModel` subscription made while the model is stopped starts when `start()` or `resumeSubscription(..)` opens its change stream.** A call that waits for it to start, such as `waitUntilStarted()`, `ProjectionRunner.project(..)` or `SagaRunner.run(..)`, hangs when it runs between `stop()` and `start()` on the thread that later calls `start()`. See [section 16 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#16-a-native-mongodb-subscription-made-while-the-model-is-stopped-starts-with-start).
* **`SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` no longer skips an event whose action keeps failing, and `SpringMongoSubscription` loses its `protected` constructor.** With a `RetryStrategy` that gives up, the subscription stops at that event. After lost history with restarting turned off, subscribe again instead of pausing and resuming. `subscribe(..)` on a shut-down model throws `IllegalStateException`. See [section 19 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#19-springmongosubscriptionmodel-reads-its-own-cursor-and-a-quiet-durable-subscription-saves-its-position).
* **A durable MongoDB subscription that receives no events now writes its checkpoint once a minute, on both stacks.** That covers `DurableSubscriptionModel` over a blocking MongoDB model, and `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` over `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel` with or without a reactor catch-up model in between. Widen such a subscription's filter and keep its id, and it no longer receives the earlier events the old filter didn't match. See [section 19 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#19-springmongosubscriptionmodel-reads-its-own-cursor-and-a-quiet-durable-subscription-saves-its-position).
* **A test that stubs `changeStream(..)` on a mocked `ReactiveMongoOperations` to make `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel` fail no longer reaches a subscription with an id.** The model opens that change stream from `getCollection(..)`, so stub that as well. See [section 19 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#19-springmongosubscriptionmodel-reads-its-own-cursor-and-a-quiet-durable-subscription-saves-its-position).
* **A `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel` subscription from `StartAt.now()` or `StartAt.subscriptionModelDefault()` starts from the `subscribe(..)` call, not from when its change stream opens.** One made while the model is stopped now receives the events written between `subscribe(..)` and `start()`. With `restartSubscriptionsOnChangeStreamHistoryLost` off, one stopped for longer than the oplog keeps history stops with an error log instead of skipping events. See [section 21 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call).
* **A `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` subscription from `StartAt.now()` over a model that is not a `SubscriptionModel` starts from what that model's `globalCheckpointAsOfNow()` answers for the `subscribe(..)` call.** One registered while the model is stopped now receives the events written between `subscribe(..)` and its start. A `globalCheckpointAsOfNow()` that always fails or never answers keeps such a subscription from starting, and an answer of `Mono.empty()` gives it the 0.33.0 start. See [section 21 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call).
* **Over a model that manages named subscriptions, a `StartAt.dynamic(..)` that answers `StartAt.now()` also starts from `globalCheckpointAsOfNow()`, when it subscribes after a cancel of the id read a stored checkpoint to delete and before that checkpoint is written back.** A `globalCheckpointAsOfNow()` that always fails or never answers keeps it from starting here too, and `Mono.empty()` gives it the 0.33.0 start. See [section 21 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call).
* **Over a model that manages named subscriptions, `isPaused(..)`, a pause, a resume or a `start(false)` can act differently from 0.33.0 for a subscription that waits for a checkpoint to be written back.** That is a `StartAt.dynamic(..)` subscription made after a cancel of the id read a stored checkpoint to delete and before that checkpoint is written back. Call `start(..)` on a stopped `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel` before you subscribe, and resume a subscription that is left paused. See [section 21 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call).
* **A reactor `CheckpointAwareSubscriptionModel` of your own must implement `globalCheckpointAsOfNow()`, which has no default implementation.** Answer where your feed was when the method was called, or fail the returned `Mono` when you can't tell. A model that wraps another passes the call on to it. See [section 21 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call).
* **Over a subscription model of your own, `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel.subscribe(..)` throws what that model's `subscriptionIds()` throws, or what its `isRunning(..)` or `isPaused(..)` throw other than `UnknownSubscriptionException`.** Make `subscriptionIds()` answer, and answer `false` from the other two for an id you don't hold. See [section 21 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call).
* **The reactor `cancelSubscription(..)` on `CancellableSubscriptions` and `DcbSubscriptionModel`, and the reactor `DcbSubscriptions.cancel(..)`, return `Mono<Void>`.** A class that implements either interface stops compiling, and `UpgradeToOccurrent_0_34` rewrites most Java ones. Recompile code that calls the method. Wait for the `Mono` when a later subscribe of the same id must start from its own `StartAt`. See [section 23 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#23-a-reactor-cancelsubscription-returns-a-mono-that-completes-once-the-stored-state-is-deleted).
* **`waitUntilStarted()` of a `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel` subscription that is cancelled, paused or stopped before it has started fails with `CancellationException`, where 0.33.0 never ended that wait.** Handle it where another part of your application may cancel, pause or stop the subscription. See [section 23 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#23-a-reactor-cancelsubscription-returns-a-mono-that-completes-once-the-stored-state-is-deleted).
* **A `ReactorDurableSubscriptionModel.subscribe(..)` from `StartAt.subscriptionModelDefault()`, over a model that manages named subscriptions such as `ReactorMongoSubscriptionModel`, returns without waiting for storage.** What it threw once it had read where to start now fails `waitUntilStarted()` instead. `DuplicateSubscriptionIdException` can come from either, so handle it in both places. See [section 25 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#25-a-durable-reactor-subscribe-from-the-model-default-returns-without-waiting-for-storage), [section 21](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#21-a-reactive-mongodb-subscription-started-at-the-present-starts-from-the-subscribe-call) and [#1193](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/1193).
* **A reactor catch-up subscription can deliver an event twice when it was written during the replay.** A handler that tolerates a repeat needs no change. If yours doesn't, make it safe to run twice before you upgrade. There is no recipe. See [section 9 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#9-a-reactor-catch-up-subscription-can-deliver-a-concurrent-write-twice).
* **A position catch-up that stores its position while it replays can deliver more events twice than in 0.33.0, and fails after 4 replays in a row when MongoDB's oplog has dropped the position where live delivery starts.** Make the handler safe to run twice, and size the oplog for the longest rebuild. A `CheckpointStorage` of your own that reads a `position:` checkpoint with `Long.parseLong` fails on the new form, so read it with `GlobalCheckpoint.parse(..)`. See [section 26 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#26-a-position-catch-up-stores-the-live-start-it-read-before-the-replay).
* **A blocking `CatchupSubscriptionModel` or `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` from a time position, `StartAtTime.offsetDateTime(..)` included, now also delivers the events stored at exactly that time.** If you pass the time of the last event you handled, that event arrives again. Fixes behaviour shipped in 0.1.1. See [section 17 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#17-startattimeoffsetdatetime-includes-the-events-stored-at-that-time).
* **A blocking time-based catch-up can deliver more events twice than in 0.33.0, and fails after 4 replays in a row when MongoDB's oplog has dropped the position where live delivery starts.** Make the handler safe to run twice, and size the oplog for the longest time replay. See [section 28 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#28-a-time-based-catch-up-reads-its-live-start-before-the-replay).
* **Two calls on a blocking `CatchupSubscriptionModel` or `StreamCatchupSubscriptionModel` now throw where 0.33.0 went ahead.** A `subscribe(..)` with the id of a subscription made through it that is running or paused throws `DuplicateSubscriptionIdException` at the call, also while its replay still runs. A `resumeSubscription(id)` or `start(true)` that would replay from a catch-up's stored position throws `IllegalStateException` when the model under the catch-up can't resume a subscription from a given position. Catch the first where you subscribe. For the second, give the catch-up a model that implements `RepositionableSubscriptions` or wraps one, such as `DurableSubscriptionModel` over either MongoDB model. See [section 29 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#29-a-blocking-subscription-that-resumes-from-another-nodes-catch-up-position-replays-from-it).
* **`RepositionableSubscriptions.findIn(..)` no longer finds a blocking `CatchupSubscriptionModel` whose wrapped model can't reposition.** In 0.33.0 it found the model, and `resumeSubscription(id, startAt)` then threw `UnsupportedOperationException`. See [section 29 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#29-a-blocking-subscription-that-resumes-from-another-nodes-catch-up-position-replays-from-it).
* **`CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel.start(..)` and `resumeSubscription(..)` log a failure for a competing subscription and try again, instead of throwing it.** A caller that caught the exception to call again no longer needs to. Call `isRunning(id)` and `isPaused(id)` to learn whether a subscription runs. See [section 18 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#18-a-competing-consumers-start-and-resumesubscription-log-a-failure-and-return).
* **`CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel.isRunning()` says whether the competing consumer model is started, not what the model it wraps says.** If you read it as "this node delivers events", call `isRunning(id)` for each subscription. A `ManualStartSubscriptionModel` that wraps it answers the same way. See [section 20 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#20-a-competing-consumers-isrunning-says-whether-the-model-is-started).
* **A competing subscription you paused on the model a `CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel` wraps now runs again on the competing consumer model's next `start(..)`, lease grant or resume.** Pause it through the competing consumer model's own `pauseSubscription(..)` instead. See [section 20 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#20-a-competing-consumers-isrunning-says-whether-the-model-is-started).
* **A new `CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel` over a model that isn't running, such as a `SpringMongoSubscriptionModel` built with `autoStartup(false)`, runs a competing subscription only after its own `start(..)`.** Call `start()` on the `CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel`. Its constructor now calls `isRunning()` on the model it wraps and throws what that call throws. See [section 22 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#22-a-competing-consumer-over-a-wrapped-model-that-is-not-running-waits-for-its-own-start).
* **A `CompetingConsumerSubscriptionModel` whose wrapped model throws from its own `shutdown()` now lets its leases expire.** `shutdown()` throws that failure and gives up no lease, and the MongoDB lease strategies stop refreshing the leases, so another node can take the subscriptions over after the lease time, 20 seconds by default. In 0.33.0 the node kept its leases and went on delivering. Call `shutdown()` again once the wrapped model can shut down. See [section 24 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.34.0.md#24-a-competing-consumer-whose-wrapped-model-fails-to-shut-down-lets-its-leases-expire).

### Changelog 0.33.0 (2026-08-16)

#### Highlights

* A flow saga step can wait on a condition instead of a single event type, so `on(allOf(...))` and `on(anyOf(...))` express an alternative or a combined count that `join` could not. `join` is deprecated in their favour.
* A flow saga can cap how many events it keeps for the step an instance is waiting in, with `stepWindow(int)`.
* A saga or a subscription that declares a sealed event type now receives the concrete events stored under it, and one that declares a type whose subtypes cannot be found is refused when it is built rather than silently receiving nothing.
* A competing consumer's checkpoint write is now stamped with its lease version, so a node that has lost its lease can no longer move the checkpoint backwards. The Spring Boot starter wires that up for you.
* A materialized view batches its store calls during a catch-up replay, so a history of N events over K projection keys costs about one read and one write per key instead of per event.

#### Changes

* **A saga that declares a sealed event type now receives the concrete events stored under it, which fixes behaviour the saga DSL shipped in 0.32.0.** `startsOn(OrderEvent.class)`, `evolve`, `react`, `step.on(OrderEvent.class, ...)` and `event(OrderEvent.class, 2)` all asked for the supertype's own CloudEvent type and nothing else, so a saga declaring a sealed interface or an abstract class received nothing at all. A saga now asks for every concrete type its sealed types permit. A saga that already declares concrete types is unaffected. See [ADR 124](doc/architecture/decisions/0124-a-saga-expands-a-declared-sealed-event-type.md) and [#743](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/743).
* **A subscription that declares a sealed event type now also asks for that type's own CloudEvent type, which changes behaviour `@Subscription`, `@StreamSubscription`, `@SynchronousSubscription` and `@DcbSubscription` shipped.** The filter named only the concrete types the sealed type permits, so an event stored as a direct instance of the declared type never matched. Adding a type can only widen what matches, so a subscription that only hit this gap keeps working. One kind of declaration is refused instead of accepted now, a sealed hierarchy that is reopened below the declared type, which 0.32.0 accepted. See [section 10 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.33.0.md#10-a-saga-or-subscription-declaring-a-supertype-event-is-refused) and [ADR 124](doc/architecture/decisions/0124-a-saga-expands-a-declared-sealed-event-type.md).
* **A saga can now select on more than event type, with `Saga.Builder.narrowingFilter(Filter)` and `Saga.Builder.replacementFilter(Filter)`.** `FlowSaga.Builder` has both, both Kotlin `saga { }` blocks expose them, and `Saga.create(...)` takes a replacement as a trailing argument. Keep either one wide enough for the saga's start events and for the events that move an instance on. See [section 10 of the upgrade guide](doc/migration/upgrading-to-0.33.0.md#10-a-saga-or-subscription-declaring-a-supertype-event-is-refused), [ADR 124](doc/architecture/decisions/0124-a-saga-expands-a-declared-sealed-event-type.md) and [#751](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/751).
  * A narrowing filter is combined with the filter derived from the saga's event types, so the saga keeps asking for its own types and also requires your condition on subject, source, data or time. On a flow saga it also changes what a guard reading `received.none(...)` sees.
  * A replacement filter is used instead of deriving one. That is how you run a saga over an event hierarchy whose concrete types cannot all be found, for example under a `CloudEventTypeMapper` of your own that maps a whole hierarchy onto a single CloudEvent type.
* A flow saga reaction can ask whether an event type has arrived, with `received.none(Rejected.class)` or `received.any(Rejected.class)`, instead of fetching the whole list and testing it for emptiness. Kotlin gains a reified `none<T>()` beside the `any<T>()` it already had.
* **A flow saga step can now wait on a condition instead of only a single event type or a `join`.** `on(StepCondition, then)` expresses an alternative ("wait for either two approvals or one rejection") or a mixed count and alternative that `join` could not. Build one from `event(type[, count][, predicate])` checks combined with `allOf(...)` and `anyOf(...)`. A condition is a plain value, so you can reuse it across `on(...)` calls and mix it with classic `on(Class, ...)` branches in the same step. Kotlin gets matching `event<T>`, `allOf`, `anyOf` and `on(condition, then)`. `join` and Kotlin's `expect<T>` are deprecated in its favour, with no forced migration. See [ADR 120](doc/architecture/decisions/0120-a-step-condition-is-a-monotone-matcher-tree.md), [ADR 123](doc/architecture/decisions/0123-a-step-conditions-counts-are-carried-so-the-steps-events-can-be-dropped.md) and [#707](https://github.com/johanhaleby/occurrent/issues/707).
  * The reaction reads the events received since its step was entered, the same ones the condition counted, so a count it takes matches the count that fired it. `received.initiating(...)` still reaches the start event, and a guard and a `timeout`'s `onExpiry` still read the whole retained history.
  * Two `allOf` checks that match the same event are refused when the saga is built, so `allOf(A.class, A.class)` asks you for `event(A.class, 2)` instead.
  * A check's predicate must answer the same way for the same event every time. It is run again over the step's events whenever the count cannot be read from the instance's state, which is the case for a predicate with no name, for a declaration that changed, and for every count during a replay.
* **A flow saga can now cap how many events it keeps for the step an instance is waiting in, with `FlowSaga.Builder.stepWindow(int)` (`stepWindow(...)` in the Kotlin `saga { }` block).** `historyWindow` only ever limited what was kept from earlier steps, so an instance waiting in one step while a large number of correlated events arrived kept every one of them, and the 0.31.0 entry that introduced `historyWindow` was wrong to say the retained state does not grow without bound. `stepWindow` is unbounded by default.
